'How to get them:\n■ List A & B: You may purchase one List A and one List B license per year, or two List B licenses per year.\nThese licenses are available through the primary draw, secondary draw, as leftover licenses and over-the-counter as available.\n■ List C: You may purchase as many List C licenses per year that you’d like, in addition to any other licenses you hold.\nThese are available through the primary draw, secondary draw, as leftover licenses and over-the-counter as available.\n■ Example: If you get a List B license in the primary draw, you can get either a List A or List B license in the secondary draw, and\nmultiple List C licenses as leftover or over-the-counter licenses.' | None | None

'Get The Lead Out\nColorado Department of Public Health & Environment and Colorado Parks and Wildlife'
'Go to cdphe.colorado.gov/lead-health and click "For the public" to see the factsheets on Lead in Harvested Wild Game and Lead in Indoor Shooting and Firing Ranges.'
'From the field to the table, ensuring your Additionally, lead-based ammunition can cause adverse impacts on nontarget wildlife from lead ingestion while scavenging. Hunters themselves are conservationists,\nnext harvest is safe for your family and maintaining species populations and protecting habitats. By switching to lead-free ammunition, hunters can support wildlife by reducing the risk of lead poisoning to nontarget\nnontarget wildlife is important. Health risks wildlife such as eagles and other raptors. For more information visit cpw.state.co.us/lead-free.\ndue to lead poisoning from consuming game\nTo help reduce human and wildlife health impacts, you can:\nmeat are low for most people; however,\n1. Use lead-free shot or bullets. There are many lead-free options now available.\nswitching to lead-free ammunition may be\n2. Hunters who use lead ammunition should choose quality hunting-grade ammunition with a "bonded" or "partition" construction.\nan option that’s right for you. Science tells\n3. Use careful shot selection. Bullets that strike the lethal area (heart/lungs) are less likely to fragment than bullets that strike more heavily boned areas.\nus that ingesting lead can cause potential 4. Bury or pack out your lead-shot gut piles to avoid lead ingestion by non target wildlife.\nhuman health problems over time. 5. Avoid eating game meat from animals harvested with lead bullets if you are pregnant. Children younger than 6 should also avoid it.'

'Identifying White-Tailed Deer vs. Mule Deer'
'White-Tailed Deer Mule Deer\n■ Primary characteristic — the tail: The first thing to look for ■ Tail: rope-like tail with black tip\nis the tail. It’s broad and brown with white fringe,\nall white when tail is up ■ Antlers: fork and fork again — often very evenly — on\nolder males\n■ Antlers: consist of one main beam with three to five tines\npointing upward. Caution: Mule deer with small antlers may\nappear to have a similar antler formation. ■ Ears: large in proportion to head on both bucks and does\n■ Ears: smaller in proportion to head ■ Gait: stiff-legged bounce, tail held down\n■ Gait: a graceful lope, flag-like tail often held up\n■ Range: statewide, in mountain shrubs, foothills, com-\n■ Range: common on Eastern Plains, in streamside wooded munities\nareas and cropland along rivers'
